To address these challenges, we propose a Reliability-Aware Multi-Strategy Guidance Network (RMGNet) for thermal-guided UAV LLIE under weak optical-thermal alignment. Its Reliability-Aware Guidance Calibration Module (RAGCM) performs coarse-to-fine displacement estimation and jointly evaluates the reliability of aligned thermal guidance and the usability of degraded visible evidence. Based on calibrated guidance, degradation, and reliability cues, the Multi-Strategy Enhancement Routing Module (MSERM) generates candidates for detail preservation, highlight-protected relighting, and thermal compensation, then spatially routes their contributions. We further construct the UAV weakly aligned Optical-Thermal dataset (UWOT), the largest UAV-oriented benchmark to date for weakly aligned optical-thermal low-light enhancement, containing 5,000 triplets. Extensive experiments show that RMGNet outperforms representative single-modal and multimodal state-of-the-art methods in enhancement quality while retaining competitive computational efficiency and greater robustness to practical cross-modal misalignment. The dataset and code will be publicly available at https://github.com/mmic-lcl/Datasets-and-benchmark-code.



### What Remains After Concept Erasure? Sparse Autoencoder Probes for Auditing Diffusion Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30117v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30117v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 06:18:49+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 06:18:49+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhuan Shi, Lu Wei, Yuta Nakashima, Noa Garcia, Golnoosh Farnadi
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Concept erasure aims to remove selected visual concepts from text-to-image diffusion models while preserving the generation of non-target concepts. Evaluation typically measures target suppression and non-target preservation in generated images, but a concept's absence from these outputs does not establish its removal from the model. We introduce SAE-Audit, a white-box audit using sparse autoencoder (SAE) probes to test whether activation interventions can restore target-concept generation without changing model weights or test prompts. After verifying target suppression, we compare the original and edited models using a shared SAE representation learned from original-model activations, identifying features whose target-related responses weaken after erasure. We inject sparse directions derived from the original-model responses into the edited model, keeping these directions fixed across test prompts, and evaluate recovery on held-out scene families. Experiments span six erasure methods and four domains: objects, artistic styles, copyrighted content, and nudity. Interventions using a few dozen selected SAE features yield higher average recovery rates than matched random-feature interventions, with recovery concentrated in objects and nudity following cross-attention edits. Most styles and copyrighted concepts show limited recovery under the tested intervention settings. These findings show that output suppression can coexist with responsiveness to sparse concept directions supplied from the original model.



### AxE: Benchmarking Agent-Environment Generalization of Video World Models via Grounded Events
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30120v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30120v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 06:23:09+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 06:23:09+00:00
- **Authors**: Haiyu Xu, Yiyang Chen, Jiayi Zhou, Jiaming Ji, Juntao Dai, Yaodong Yang, Boyuan Chen
- **Comment**: Project page: https://AxEBench.github.io
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Edge cases are not the long tail; they are the norm. An embodied system deployed across changing environments, bodies, objects, and tasks will routinely encounter combinations that were absent from its training data. General-purpose physical intelligence therefore requires world models that reuse prior knowledge, predict consequences, and adapt with little or no additional training. Yet current evaluations of conditional video generation emphasize visual quality, prompt alignment, or isolated physical plausibility, leaving systematic generalization difficult to measure. We introduce AxE Bench, a benchmark that organizes condition changes along eight factors of the acting agent and of its environment, and pairs every original condition with controlled variants that change one side or both. WBench-plus applies the same structure to an existing multi-turn benchmark, pairing each WBench scenario with variants on the environment side, through its world setting or camera behavior, or on the agent side, through its interaction instructions. Additionally, to score generated futures, we develop an evaluation harness that combines vision-language reasoning with targeted computer-vision evidence, converting each video into temporally localized events and comparing generated events with reference events in both time and semantics. We summarize each model with the AxE Score, event Fidelity weighted by its Stability across original-variant pairs. Across twenty models, generated events match reference events far more often in timing than in content: no model exceeds a Fidelity of 13.01, the highest Stability is 0.55, and the highest AxE Score is 7.10. Together, these components turn world-model generalization from an informal aspiration into a falsifiable evaluation program for real and simulated interactions. Our project website can be found at: AxEBench.github.io



### Separating Protocol Effects from Spatio-Temporal Evidence in Person Re-Identification Re-Ranking
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30142v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30142v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 06:48:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 06:48:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Mochamad Rizal Fauzan, Doan Pham Thao Nhu, Jeslin Konthoujam, Purit Hongjirakul, Lakshay Chawla
- **Comment**: 11 pages, 7 figures, 9 tables. Code: https://github.com/rizalfanex/spatiotemporal-bayesian-reid
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Spatio-temporal priors re-rank person re-identification matches using the camera pair and the time gap, and are reported to add large gains on Market-1501. We ask how much of such a gain encodes the evaluation protocol rather than learned spatio-temporal evidence. Starting from a course-project re-ranker that adds log camera-transition and log time-gap terms to cosine similarity, we train a strong ResNet-50 baseline (Rank-1 94.3 %, mAP 86.2 % over three seeds), choose the fusion weights on an identity-disjoint validation split and evaluate on the official test set with control priors. The repository form adds 2.30 mAP points, but a one-parameter penalty on same-camera pairs alone adds 2.19: the protocol never places a true match in the query's camera, so the penalty removes distractors for free. The time term of that form is almost unused; a pair-level diagnostic traces this to its modelling the time gap given a match without dividing by the gap distribution of all candidate pairs. A likelihood-ratio prior adds 4.04 points without the penalty and 5.62 with it; shuffling the time stamps removes the extra gain, so about 3.43 points are tied to identity. The prior fixes the first-ranked match of 4.2 % of the queries and breaks it for 0.1 %. The gain shrinks as the visual baseline improves (11.9 to 5.62 points between a 70.5 % and a 86.2 % mAP model) and concentrates on queries whose true matches share the query's video sequence. The study covers one dataset, one backbone and three seeds.



### RMR-VLA: Recurrent Memory Reasoning for Vision-Language-Action Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30145v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30145v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 06:49:35+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 06:49:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Yiheng Du, Jingqi Tian, Xu Jiang, Jiexuan Zhang, Shuyu Wang, Zecheng Tang, Jian Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Long-horizon, history-dependent robotic manipulation often requires acting on visual cues that have disappeared, such as an object indicated earlier or a target hidden under a moving cup. The key challenge is to maintain what past evidence means for the next action as the scene changes. We introduce Recurrent Memory Reasoning (RMR), an explicit reasoning step that connects memory to action inside a vision-language-action model (VLA), with no external reasoning model. At each update, the VLA first infers an event from visual evidence, then revises the task state and grounds a subgoal in the current scene to guide action generation. Carrying the state and subgoal across updates lets remembered identities and task progress determine the current target, doubling RoboMME success over an otherwise identical model without recurrence. Joint training lets one VLA both reason and act; because its memory decisions are text tokens with exact likelihoods, GRPO refines them from rollout rewards without making action generation stochastic. Among the compared methods, RMR-VLA achieves the best results on RoboMME (66.46% overall, 79.83% on the Reference category) and LIBERO-Mem (90.08%).



### LeadVLA: Learning Follower-Aware Route Execution for Robot Leading
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30149v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30149v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 06:57:07+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 06:57:07+00:00
- **Authors**: Tianle Zeng, Hanjing Ye, Bingyi Xia, Yuchen Zhou, Zibo Zhang, Yufei Huang, Yonggen Ling, Hong Zhang
- **Comment**: Project page: https://louiszengCN.github.io/LeadVLA_PAGE/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Robot leading requires executing a route while continuously adapting to a designated follower. Existing robot-leading systems often mediate follower understanding and physical route execution through explicit states, planners, or modular interfaces; directly learning this coupling through end-to-end trajectory prediction can instead entangle route execution with follower response. We introduce LeadVLA, a structured multimodal policy that preserves the route as an explicit Path reference while learning a Pace for route progress and a Residual for local follower- and scene-conditioned adaptation. To make this problem trainable and measurable, we develop LeadInfra, a configurable Unreal Engine-based closed-loop data-generation infrastructure from which we instantiate an approximately 4M-sample training corpus, and LeadBench, a standardized benchmark with 108 fully held-out closed-loop episodes. LeadVLA achieves the highest task success (39.6%) among evaluated robot policies on LeadBench while maintaining strong route fidelity and follower responsiveness, and transfers to unseen real-world environments without real-world policy fine-tuning. Project Page: https://louiszengCN.github.io/LeadVLA_PAGE/



### Preserve to Compress: Beyond Low-Dimensional Latents in One-Step Diffusion Codec
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30154v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30154v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:02:49+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:02:49+00:00
- **Authors**: Chuqin Zhou, Xiaoyue Ling, Yuhang Wei, Yunuo Chen, Guo Lu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pretrained latent diffusion models bring powerful generative priors to ultra-low-bitrate image compression, but their latent spaces reflect a reconstruction--generation tradeoff. Low-dimensional latents reduce modeling cost but can discard image details, while higher-dimensional latents improve reconstruction yet make diffusion optimization harder under a fixed computational budget. Generative codecs that adopt these pretrained spaces also inherit this compromise, even though compression requires fidelity to a specific source image. In particular, low-dimensional latent interfaces can discard source details before coding and restrict how much decoded information reaches the image decoder. We propose Preserving Information for Compression and Synthesis (PICS), a one-step generative image compression framework that adapts these interfaces to preserve source information. Our Information-Preserving Adaptors (IPAs) expose richer features for coding and retain decoded information throughout reconstruction, while deferred decoder adaptation stabilizes training and helps the image decoder exploit the expanded representations. Experiments on Kodak, DIV2K, and CLIC 2020 show average BD-rate savings of 52.64% and 39.61% over StableCodec at matched LPIPS and DISTS, respectively.



### UUBench: Understanding the Unspoken toward Full-Duplex Multimodal Interaction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30157v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30157v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:03:50+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:03:50+00:00
- **Authors**: Ye Pan, Chenfei Liao, Bin Ren, Hanqian Li, Haocong He, Jialei Chen, Jinlin He, Kunyu Peng, Mengyu Jin, Shijie Li, Wenqian Cui, Yuanqi Yao, Yuqian Fu, Kailong Ruan, Yuan Gao, Wei Wang, Xuming Hu, Irwin King, Nicu Sebe, Xu Zheng
- **Comment**: 49 pages, 37 figures, 9 tables, including appendices. Preview version; work in progress
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Effective real-time, full-duplex multimodal interaction requires systems to understand not only what users say, but also the communicative needs conveyed through prosody, facial expressions, gestures, and context, and to generate audiovisual responses that appropriately address those needs. Existing evaluations offer limited insight into whether systems translate an understanding of such unspoken needs into effective responses. We introduce UUBench, a benchmark for assessing how well multimodal interactive systems Understand the Unspoken intent and address users' implicit communicative needs. The inherent subjectivity of implicit intent inference poses a key challenge for evaluation. To address this, we introduce Process-Oriented Scoring, which grounds the assessment of intent fulfillment in observable auditory and visual cues, such as prosody and gestures. Rather than relying solely on a single overall score, it evaluates how well a response addresses the communicative needs conveyed by each applicable primitive dimension. We aggregate the dimension-level scores according to each dimension's relevance to the annotated evidence. Beyond intent fulfillment, Outcome-Oriented Scoring evaluates the naturalness, appropriateness, and human-likeness of the reply itself under audio-only, visual-only, and audiovisual conditions. To make these judgments traceable to observable evidence, we propose a Fact-Grounded Scoring JEV module, which uses structured, timestamped audiovisual observations of the user and the digital human's reply to assess one dimension or criterion at a time. UUBench provides a foundation for assessing whether multimodal interactive systems respond effectively to meaning beyond literal wording, supporting progress toward context-aware, real-time, full-duplex interaction.



### OpenViVE: New Benchmark and Dual-Stream Co-Attention Framework for Vietnamese Visual Entailment
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30161v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30161v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:07:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:07:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Bao Quoc Trinh, Tin Van Huynh, Kiet Van Nguyen, Ngan Luu-Thuy Nguyen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual Entailment (VE) determines whether a textual hypothesis is entailed, contradicted, or unverifiable from an image. However, existing VE benchmarks are largely English-centric and do not capture Vietnamese-specific linguistic distinctions or culturally grounded visual contexts. To address this gap, we introduce OpenViVE, the first large-scale native Vietnamese VE benchmark, containing 62,268 balanced image-hypothesis pairs from 10,378 culturally grounded images. OpenViVE is constructed using an Artifact-Aware Hybrid LLM-Human Pipeline that combines constrained generation, human validation, and PMI-guided refinement. The pipeline substantially reduces hypothesis-only shortcuts, with Contradiction accuracy dropping from 96% to 66% after artifact refinement. We further evaluate a dual-stream PhoBERT-ConvNeXt framework with bidirectional co-attention to align visual evidence with Vietnamese hypotheses. The framework achieves 84.84% accuracy and 84.90% macro-F1 on the test set, while PhoBERT consistently outperforms multilingual XLM-R variants. These results highlight the importance of language-specific encoding and explicit cross-modal interaction for Vietnamese VE.



### WaDiS: Boundary-Preserving Diffusion Segmentation via Multi-Scale Wavelet Conditioning and Frequency-Aware Gating
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30164v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30164v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:11:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:11:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Qiang Gao, Yi Wang, Yong Zhang, Bin Fang, Yong Li, Lan Du, Cunjian Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Diffusion models have shown strong potential in medical image segmentation due to their generative capabilities and robustness. However, existing methods often struggle to preserve fine anatomical boundaries and to retain multi-scale structural information due to the loss of spatial and frequency details during feature encoding, particularly in complex clinical imaging conditions. This work presents WaDiS, a wavelet-powered conditional latent diffusion framework for medical image segmentation that addresses these limitations through multi-scale wavelet conditioning and frequency-aware information filtering for boundary preservation. The model adopts a multi-scale wavelet conditioning encoder to retain rich structural features, leveraging its lossless nature to better preserve semantic priors critical to the diffusion process. In addition, a Frequency-Aware Gating Block (FAGB) is introduced into the skip connections of the diffusion U-Net, enabling adaptive spectral filtering and residual fusion to better emphasize boundary structures while reducing irrelevant content. To improve generalization across clinical domains, we adopt a structure-preserving color augmentation strategy during training. Specifically, global color statistics are transferred between samples in the LAB color space, allowing the model to decouple appearance from structure and focus more on anatomical shape rather than color bias. Experiments on five benchmark datasets demonstrate that WaDiS consistently outperforms existing diffusion-based and conventional segmentation methods, with notable improvements in boundary accuracy, robustness to noise, and generalization under color variation. The code for this work will be made publicly available upon acceptance.



### Multimodal View Training: Learning More Through Different Modalities
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30165v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30165v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:11:55+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:11:55+00:00
- **Authors**: Devang Kulshreshtha, Priyanka Kaswan, Minh Pham, Chinmay Hegde, Niv Cohen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Can a language model learn more from the same information simply by encountering it in different forms? We answer this in the affirmative by introducing Multimodal View Training (MVT), a simple training approach that presents identical information through text, speech, and images, without requiring new facts or supervision. Starting from a text sample, MVT generates corresponding audio and rendered-text image views, and trains a multimodal language model to produce the same target output across all three views. To further bring these benefits to text-only large language models (LLMs), we train lightweight connector modules to pretrained audio and vision encoders, allowing the LLM to learn from multimodal inputs while retaining its text-only architecture at inference. Across question answering, machine unlearning, and knowledge editing, adaptive MVT improves QA recall by +15.2 percentage points, forget quality by +40.7 points, and editing generality by +25.2 points over text-only training. Combining MVT with standard augmentation further improves QA and editing generality. Our theoretical analysis illustrates how training across modalities can shift weight updates toward shared factual content and reduce their reliance on features specific to each view.



### PathStream: Towards Streaming Diagnostic Copilots for Whole-Slide Pathology
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30167v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30167v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:13:20+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:13:20+00:00
- **Authors**: Dankai Liao, YuFeng Wu, Ziyue Wang, Guanxi Liu, Tianyi Zhang, Zeyu Liu, Linghan Cai, Yueming Jin
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pathologists reach a diagnosis by integrating morphological evidence across regions and magnifications along viewing trajectories. Existing pathology AI rarely reflects this process: models predict from selected image regions, answer questions within fixed visual contexts, or navigate slides autonomously. We formalize pathologist-led streaming diagnosis, in which the pathologist controls navigation while a copilot selectively describes incoming views and synthesizes accumulated evidence into a final diagnosis. We record five pathologists' viewing trajectories on 1,791 TCGA whole-slide images (WSIs) spanning ten organs, with response labels, morphological descriptions, and session-level findings and diagnoses. We construct PathStreamBench to assess response timing, morphological description, and session-level diagnosis, alongside auxiliary video understanding. We introduce PathStream, which jointly learns streaming responses and diagnosis through two-stage training, with a diagnosis-attention loss guiding attention during diagnosis to relevant visual and textual evidence accumulated as the examination unfolds. PathStream outperforms 14 vision-language models and three domain-fine-tuned baselines on the core tasks, achieving a trajectory WSI Relevance score of 77.87, surpassing the strongest domain-fine-tuned baseline's 62.76. Without additional training, its diagnostic capabilities generalize to new educational pathology videos, three external centers, and an unseen organ.



### GUI-Q: Task-Sensitive Granularity and Relation-Preserving Quantization for GUI Agents
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30170v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30170v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:15:53+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:15:53+00:00
- **Authors**: Tianqi Li, Yang Wang, Xin He, Le Zhang, Yun Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Graphical user interface (GUI) agents enable automated interactions with mobile, web, and desktop interfaces. However, their large memory footprint and repeated model calls hinder deployment on resource-constrained devices, motivating post-training quantization (PTQ) without full retraining. Quantization can degrade GUI response accuracy and distort the instruction-vision affinities and local spatial relations needed for GUI target selection. Small coordinate errors can cause missed clicks, while early action errors can alter subsequent interface states and disrupt task execution. Local reconstruction error alone does not directly assess whether these responses and relations are preserved. We propose GUI-Q, a PTQ framework that targets response and relation preservation with 4-bit weights and 16-bit activations (W4A16). Task-Sensitive Granularity Allocation (TSGA) allocates finer groups under a scale-byte budget by combining response sensitivity with the measured reduction in activation-weighted distortion. Relation-Preserving Symmetry Selection (RPSS) then selects symmetric or asymmetric grids to preserve instruction-vision affinities and local spatial relations in GUIs. Across three GUI-capable models, GUI-Q improves mean grounding TargetHit by 8.04 to 12.55 points over the strongest evaluated 4-bit configurations, while reducing static peak graphics processing unit (GPU) memory by 49.9% to 56.2% relative to bfloat16 (BF16). Fixed-output replay with packed 4-bit execution provides 1.7 to 2.8 times model-call speedups. The code will be released.



### Audio-Visual Rearrangement
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30171v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.MM, cs.SD
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30171v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:15:54+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:15:54+00:00
- **Authors**: Ruohao Guo, Jiatong Ye, Chenwei Dong, Jinxing Zhou, Dantong Niu, Guangyao Li, Zhenbo Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We introduce Audio-Visual Rearrangement (AVR), a novel task in which an agent hears a spoken request, retrieves a suitable object, and delivers it to the requesting user or an instruction-specified destination. AVR requires the agent to understand the spoken request and acoustically localize the speaker for delivery. To support this task, we construct a dataset covering three query types: explicit target specification, speaker-relative spatial references, and implicit user intent, spanning eight subtypes with diverse object selection and delivery requirements. Moreover, we propose a framework that integrates a motion-aware spatial audio module into an audio-visual large language model to encode spatial cues from the spoken request and update them as the agent moves. We further incorporate auxiliary supervision for speech understanding, spatial audio perception, and visual grounding through multitask instruction tuning to improve action prediction. Experiments establish baseline performance in both seen and unseen environments, laying the groundwork for future research on embodied intelligence and human-robot interaction. The code will be publicly available soon.



### OmniSenMo: A Unified Multisensory Language Model for Generalizable Human Motion Estimation, Understanding, and Prediction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30176v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30176v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:22:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:22:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Cheng Guo, Qiming Cao, Pu Perry Wang, Pu Wang, Hongfei Xue
- **Comment**: Project website: https://ch3ngguo.github.io/omnisenmo/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) estimate, understand, and predict human motion, but mainly from RGB cameras. Depth, LiDAR, mmWave, and WiFi complement RGB under poor lighting, through occlusions, and in privacy-sensitive applications, but existing multisensory models cover only some of these sensing tasks. Covering all of them from any sensor subset is challenging, since the tokens of heterogeneous sensors are semantically inconsistent and spatially misaligned, paired multisensory data are scarce, and the tasks need different temporal granularities. We present OmniSenMo, a multisensory language model that takes the human skeleton as a common reference for all sensors. Its skeleton-grounded motion representation turns each sensor into joint tokens, one per body joint, so that any sensor subset is fused joint by joint and expressed in the motion vocabulary that the language model learned from motion capture and text. We pretrain it on all five sensors generated by our multisensory simulator, and Multi-Granularity Observation gives each task the frames it needs. On a cross-action split of MM-Fi, the same model with all five sensors outperforms the models compared on each task, including LEO-Fuse in pose estimation (84.1 vs. 89.0 mm MPJPE) and HoloLLM in unseen-action recognition (69.2% vs. 42.8%). Even without RGB, it outperforms both methods that use RGB, and in a cross-dataset validation on XRF55, it recognizes unseen actions far better than HoloLLM.



### Visual-RFT++: Extending Visual Reinforcement Fine-Tuning to Multimodal Tool Use
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30193v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30193v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:36:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:36:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Ziyu Liu, Zeyi Sun, Xiaoyi Dong, Yuhang Cao, Haodong Duan, Dahua Lin, Jiaqi Wang, Yuhang Zang
- **Comment**: Our code is available at https://github.com/Liuziyu77/Visual-RFT
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reinforcement Fine-Tuning (RFT) has recently become a central paradigm for advancing Large Reasoning Models (LRMs), as demonstrated by systems such as OpenAI's o3 and the R1 series. Despite significant progress in LLMs, how to leverage RFT to unify visual perception, step-by-step reasoning, and agentic tool use into one coherent training paradigm remains an open challenge. In this work, we present Visual Reinforcement Fine-Tuning++ (Visual-RFT++), a unified framework that extends RFT to both visual perception tasks and multimodal agentic workflows, enabling LVLMs to reason with images, act upon images, and evaluate their own outputs using verifiable reward. With Visual-RFT++, open-source LVLMs not only show significant gains on diverse visual perception tasks, but also acquire agentic abilities such as web searching for real-time information and code-based image manipulation (e.g., cropping, rotation, and analysis). Across visual perception tasks, including fine-grained classification, few-shot detection, reasoning grounding, and open-vocabulary detection, Visual-RFT++ delivers strong gains over SFT, including a +24.3 percentage points improvement in one-shot fine-grained classification and +21.0 percentage points on COCO's 4-shot setting and +15.4 percentage points on LVIS with around several hundred samples. To evaluate agentic behaviors, we introduce the Multimodal Agentic Tool Bench (MAT) with MAT-Search and MAT-Coding. Visual-RFT++ surpasses its baseline by +18.6 F1 / +13.0 EM percentage points on MAT-Coding and +10.3 F1 / +8.7 EM percentage points on MAT-Search. It also shows gains of 29.3 F1 and 25.9 EM percentage points on multi-hop QA benchmarks such as 2Wiki and HotpotQA, demonstrating strong cross-modal generalization. Visual-RFT++ introduces a data-efficient and reward-driven framework that strengthens both visual reasoning and agentic adaptability.



### Quantization for Action, Execution for Deployment: Joint Precision and Execution Selection for World Action Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30195v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30195v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:38:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:38:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Tianqi Li, Xin He, Xue Geng, Le Zhang, Ming-Ming Cheng, Yun Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World action models guide robots by predicting the effects of actions or generating commands from observations. A model may compare hundreds of candidate futures before acting, repeatedly reading weights that must fit in device memory. Post-training quantization reduces the number of bits used to store pretrained weights, but a smaller model is not necessarily a deployable one: temporary tensors can exceed graphics processing unit (GPU) memory limits, while even small prediction errors can change the selected action. This creates two coupled challenges: identifying bit assignments that preserve task performance and determining which of them meet real execution constraints, without exhausting costly task trials. We present PTQ4WAM, a framework for choosing layer-wise bit assignments and execution routes under memory, latency and task-evaluation budgets. Calibration measurements guide candidate proposals, optionally with a language model agent. We profile full-model routes on the GPU before spending task trials on feasible assignments. Fusing group rescaling and reading visual history in place can make an otherwise infeasible assignment deployable. Across 15 workloads from four model families, the median task score is 99.2% of the corresponding 32-bit floating-point (FP32) score. On fixed GPU replays, the median speedup over FP32 is 2.36 times and the median reduction in peak allocated memory is 71.5%. The code will be released soon.



### ToWo-VTON: Take-Off Wear-On Virtual Try-On via Topology-aware Decoupling
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30203v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30203v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:42:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:42:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhenchen Wan, Tianxi Chen, Runqi Lin, Tianyi Bo, Ce Chen, Jiaxin Huang, Yanwu Xu, Tongliang Liu, Mingming Gong
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Virtual Try-On (VTON) aims to synthesize garment transfer without physical trials. Although recent methods perform well on upper-body garments, they still struggle in lower-body and full-body scenarios with complex occlusions and large silhouette changes. To address this limitation, we introduce ToWo (Take-Off Wear-On), a two-stage paradigm that decouples body structure estimation from garment synthesis. Take-Off first generates a tight-fitting body proxy that provides estimated structural guidance, and Wear-On then synthesizes the target garment conditioned on this prior. This decoupling reduces source-garment interference, supporting garment placement under substantial silhouette variations. Built on this paradigm, we propose ToWo-VTON, a unified topology-aware framework with structure-aware supervision. We construct the Body-Fitting DressCode (BFDC) dataset via body-aware diffusion and VLM-based filtering to provide body-proxy supervision across upper-, lower-, and full-body cases. We instantiate ToWo-VTON on both diffusion-based and MLLM-guided backbones. For diffusion-based models, we fine-tune on BFDC using the body proxy as the person input. For the MLLM-guided model, we use native backbone conditioning for the body proxy and target garment, and add Garment Boosting Attention (GBA) to preserve garment details. Experiments on VITON-HD and DressCode demonstrate improvements over the evaluated baselines, with KID reductions of 26.1% and 58.6% for diffusion-based and MLLM-guided implementations, respectively. The combined BFDC-training and Take-Off/Wear-On strategy also produces more coherent limb layouts and garment boundaries across the evaluated garment categories.



### Benchmarking Geospatial Foundation Models under Calibrated Label Errors
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30215v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30215v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:50:04+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:50:04+00:00
- **Authors**: Mollah Md Saif, Showrin Rahman, Anindya Majumder, Sowad Rahman, Md. Golam Rabiul Alam
- **Comment**: 100 pages (15 pages main text and references, 85 pages appendix), 78 figures, 68 tables. Extended version including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Earth-observation labels from maps, older products or hurried annotation arrive misaligned, coarse, loosely outlined or incomplete. Geospatial foundation models (GFMs) are nevertheless benchmarked with labels taken as correct. A practitioner therefore cannot tell which encoder to choose when the labels are imperfect. We present a benchmark protocol for fine-tuning under such labels. Four spatially structured error types are calibrated to equal label mIoU and applied identically to every model and seed. Every contrast is paired and read against an equivalence margin of $\pm 3$ points and a minimum detectable effect. We apply it to nine encoders, six decoder heads and ten datasets. In an exploratory reading at a matched learning rate the satellite-pretrained and web-pretrained DINOv3 encoders are equivalent within the margin on 8 of 9 datasets. Multispectral GFMs and an ImageNet ResNet-50 are equivalent within the margin on about half of them. Labels with random offsets cost the least at equal label quality. Under other decoder heads the verdicts mostly hold on FLAIR-2 and are decoder dependent on HLS Burn Scars. On four of five real label pairs the real label costs more than its matched simulated type. No choice of pretraining data makes an encoder consistently more robust, so the encoder is better chosen by its accuracy under imperfect labels.



### RoomTokens: Masked Diffusion over Structured Tokens for Indoor Scene Synthesis
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30219v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30219v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:51:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:51:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Xiaohao Sun, Angel X. Chang
- **Comment**: Project page: https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/RoomToken/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Generating 3D indoor scenes requires a representation that captures object semantics, instance identity, height structure, and orientation while respecting room geometry. Methods that model scenes as sequences of object vectors learn spatial constraints implicitly, leaving collisions and boundary violations to losses or post-processing; semantic-map methods make occupancy explicit, but collapse instance and directional structure. We introduce RoomTokens, a sliced structured-token representation in which each token stores semantic class, instance-color identity, and facing field. This representation allows a scene to be generated as a discrete grid while preserving cues for instance grouping, orientation estimation, and 3D extent prediction. To learn the RoomToken distribution, we propose MDIRT (Masked Diffusion over RoomTokens), a masked discrete diffusion model that denoises semantic/instance-color/facing channels jointly with a shared cross-slice backbone. Architectural tokens are clamped as explicit floor- plan constraints, so the model samples furniture layouts that respect room geometry while retaining instance and object-attribute structure. Experiments on 3D-FRONT show that RoomTokens with MDIRT improve layout fidelity and physical consistency over sequence/vector- and map-based methods, suggesting structured spatial tokens as a practical choice for discrete 3D scene synthesis.



### EditGRN: Inversion-Free Image Editing by Source-Target Generative Synchronization
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30224v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30224v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 07:54:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 07:54:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiahuan Wang, Jianan Chen, Jian Han, Rui Li, Guangming Lu, Wenjie Pei
- **Comment**: 16 pages,10 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Text-guided image editing faces a persistent trade-off between preserving source content and realizing coherent target semantics. We explore this challenge through training- and inversion-free image editing based on discrete generative models. Existing inversion-free discrete editors primarily rely on post-hoc probability correction, creating a disconnect between local token-level adjustments and globally coherent semantic generation. To bridge this gap, we introduce EditGRN, a training- and inversion-free framework with trajectory-wide source-target generative synchronization in discrete space. EditGRN adopts a dual-branch design---the source branch constructs source references and the target branch generates the target image-enabling trajectory-wide synchronization between the two. Exploiting GRN's ability to recover clean tokens from arbitrary binary corruptions, the source branch directly constructs corrupted source states at each refinement step to extract useful editing features, without recovering a specific source-generation trajectory. The target branch synchronizes with the source branch through the same corruption schedule, fusing the corresponding source context with editable representations intra-model. By synchronizing source context with target generation along the entire trajectory, our approach faithfully preserves the background while producing high-quality edits that blend seamlessly with the surrounding scene through intra-model fusion. PIE-Bench show state-of-the-art performance on background preservation and text alignment across diverse editing tasks against continuous and discrete baselines.



### PixelOne: From Options to Pixels in a Single Forward for GUI Grounding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30235v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30235v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 08:04:09+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 08:04:09+00:00
- **Authors**: Kanghui Tian, Shuai Dong, Yuhan Zhu, Congcong Wang, Ji Zhao, Tianxiang Jiang, Zhixiong Zhang, Shengyuan Ding, Yi Wang, Jiaqi Wang, Yu Qiao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: GUI grounding requires precise clicks at low latency. Recent models such as GUI-Decisions and GUI-Swift predict coordinates in one forward pass using fixed global bins with local refinement. However, screenshot downsampling removes detail needed to locate small controls, while fixed bins cover larger pixel ranges at higher resolutions. We introduce PixelOne, which transfers spatial knowledge from a search teacher to a student that predicts clicks directly from screenshots at native resolution. The teacher uses VideoJEV's text-option scorer to select rows and columns and progressively zoom into candidate regions. The student inherits the teacher's parameters and matches its distributions over explored regions through spatial distillation alongside box supervision. At inference, the same decision head selects a visual token, and a lightweight pixel head predicts the click within it, all in one forward pass. A click-aligned loss encourages pixels inside the target box to score above those outside. Across four benchmarks, PixelOne-Pro-35B-A3B achieves 83.8% mean accuracy, the highest among the evaluated single-forward grounders, with a mean latency of 0.43 s per target.



### AIGI-26: A Benchmark for AI-Generated Image Detection Across Diverse Modern Generators
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30236v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30236v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 08:04:13+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 08:04:13+00:00
- **Authors**: Qishuai Ding, Yifeng Wang, Shichuang Xie, Yanli Ren, Guorui Feng
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Existing AI-generated image (AIGI) detection benchmarks predominantly rely on earlier synthesis systems with limited content diversity and conspicuous artifacts, failing to fully reflect the detection challenges posed by high-fidelity images from contemporary models. We introduce AIGI-26, a benchmark of 253,200 images covering 26 recent open- and closed-source generators. Its novel, diverse, and heterogeneous sources provide a basis for systematically evaluating detection methods on modern generated imagery. Additionally, to capture subtle statistical discrepancies across complementary forensic views, we propose the multi-aspect forensic detection network (MAFDNet). Its fixed forensic operators expose complementary texture, local prediction, resampling, and chromatic responses, summarized by a hierarchical encoder using multiscale means and standard deviations. In parallel, a spectral branch captures Fourier log-magnitude and neighboring-frequency phase variation across radial bands and directional axes. Fusing these descriptors combines local inconsistencies with global spectral organization for authenticity detection. Trained on 12,660 AIGI-26 images, MAFDNet achieves 88.5% accuracy and 94.9% average precision on the 240,540-image test set, with only 1.04M parameters and 1.39 GFLOPs per $256\times256$ input. MAFDNet also demonstrates effective cross-dataset generalization on DailyBench and Treasure-37.



### TriSegAgent: Context-Efficient 3D Annotation with MLLMs via Tri-Planes
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30240v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30240v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 08:06:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 08:06:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Qingqiao Hu, Jialin Li, Meilong Xu, Kehan Qi, Yipeng Zhang, Lin Tian, Hongwei Bran Li, Klaus Mueller, Xiaoling Hu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Interactive segmentation models turn a few clicks into a segmentation prediction across domains, so annotators only decide where the prediction still needs correction. Annotation thus becomes a loop of inspecting the prediction and clicking again. Each step of this loop is a visual judgment, which suits general-purpose multimodal large language model (MLLM) agents. In this work, we focus on letting these agents run this loop on 3D volumes. However, the MLLM in such an agent takes only 2D images as input, so the agent must observe the 3D volume in 2D. Since the loop calls the MLLM many times, this observation must be token-compact enough to keep every call cheap, yet informative enough to show error regions in the 3D prediction. We meet both needs with tri-planes, the three orthogonal planes that cross at one point in the volume. We show, both theoretically and empirically, that tri-planes are context-efficient yet keep sufficient detail to show error regions in the 3D prediction. Building on tri-planes, we introduce TriSegAgent, an MLLM agent that refines the 3D prediction iteratively. Within the agent, the MLLM only judges the prediction on tri-planes, while a visual harness turns each judgment into a 3D action in code. To make the loop reliable, the harness places each click at an exact voxel in the volume and checks whether to accept the 3D prediction. To evaluate the agent, we build a diverse benchmark of 690 regions of interest (ROIs) from 35 public medical datasets across three imaging modalities. With frontier MLLMs, TriSegAgent reaches an overall Dice about 10 points above the strongest baseline, and its lead is even larger on targets below 3 cm. With flash-level MLLMs, it outperforms a recent 3D segmentation agent built on a frontier MLLM.



### OCCA-Seg: Object and Channel Context Aggregation for Semantic Segmentation of Remote Sensing Images
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30267v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30267v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 08:28:41+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 08:28:41+00:00
- **Authors**: Gang Yang, Si Chen, Quan Zhou, Jiangchuan Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Semantic segmentation, which assigns a class label to each pixel, is a key task in computer vision. However, semantic segmentation of remote sensing images (RSIs) remains challenging because of complex scenes and objects at multiple scales, which limits segmentation accuracy. To address these challenges, we propose a context aggregation scheme called object and channel context aggregation (OCCA), consisting of three components: object contextual attention module (OCAM), channel contextual attention module (CCAM), and aggregation module (AM). Firstly, motivated by the observation that each pixel shares the class label of the object to which it belongs, object-level context represents each pixel through aggregating context from its corresponding object class. Secondly, because feature channels are correlated with object categories, channel-wise context models channel-wise contextual dependencies conditioned on object categories. Finally, the context information from OCAM and CCAM are fed into AM and used to weight and modulate the object context, enhancing the discriminative ability of the segmentation model. The experiments on several public remote sensing datasets including LoveDA, UAVid, ISPRS Potsdam, and Vaihingen datasets demonstrate competitive improvements over existing methods.



### RigidAnchor: Cross-View Geo-Localization for UAV via Iterative Planar Rigid Alignment
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30278v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30278v1)
