- **Authors**: Lu Han, Jingyao Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A generator can follow a spatial plan, but the benefit of supplying that plan depends on what the model can already do from text. In a controlled study with 18 paired training seeds, correcting predicted positions and sizes improves spatial intersection-over-union by 0.030, yet direct text is non-inferior at matched training compute. In a pretrained generator, a fixed rule layout improves two-object task success over the same text-only adapter by 12.50 percentage points, mostly by separating objects that already appear in the correct order. We then ask whether a more precise instruction can achieve the same separation across conditioning paths. On 288 new task families, adding a minimum-gap instruction improves success by 37.15 points with the text-only adapter, by at most 0.69 points with widely spaced regions, and by 6.94 points with tightly spaced regions. Under tight regions, 230 of 288 baseline images have the correct objects and direction yet miss the required gap, ruling out a high baseline success rate as the sole explanation for the weak text response.



### Field-Level Crop Classification via Similarity-Induced Supervision from Imperfect Labels
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30515v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30515v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 11:32:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 11:32:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Raul Lomonte Figueiredo, João Rebouças Dorea, Dário Augusto Borges Oliveira
- **Comment**: 38 pages, 21 figures, 8 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Regional-scale crop mapping is critical for food production monitoring, yet remains challenging in tropical regions, where persistent cloud cover limits optical imagery. Synthetic aperture radar (SAR) enables continuous observation throughout the agricultural cycle, but a second obstacle is label quality: national-scale land cover products exhibit systematic biases that underestimate less prevalent crops, and training directly on them inherits and amplifies their errors. This work proposes a methodology to build large-scale crop classification models from imperfect public land cover products, requiring no new field campaign, ground survey, or manual label correction. The approach combines two mechanisms. The first reduces the semantic inconsistency of the labels by shifting supervision from the sample level to the group level: a similarity-based clustering model organizes SAR time series into coherent groups, and noisy labels are used only to assign each group its most representative class. The resulting clusters are highly pure with respect to the reference classes (89.0% and 91.2% in the two study regions). The second reduces the spatial inconsistency of the predictions: a segmentation model delineates crop field boundaries from optical imagery, and patch-level predictions are consolidated by majority voting within each field, yielding spatially coherent maps. Evaluated in two Brazilian municipalities, Campo Verde (Mato Grosso) and Luís Eduardo Magalhães (Bahia), the approach consistently outperforms the noisy baseline in mean class accuracy, with gains of up to 10.14 percentage points, especially for minority crops. A state-scale analysis across Mato Grosso and Bahia shows stable behavior without further model adjustment, and the derived municipal crop areas are consistent with official IBGE/PAM statistics, an independent estimate the model never observes.



### GeoRead3D: Grounding Part Descriptions for 3D Pointing and Reorientation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30519v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30519v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 11:37:07+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 11:37:07+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhangcheng Hou, Kevin Tonkich, Tomoaki Ohtsuki
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 3 figures, 5 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Language often refers to an object by its parts, as in asking to point to a part or to turn an arbitrarily posed object so that two named parts lie in given directions. Both tasks need the direction from the object's center to the part's center, not the part's own orientation. A natural approach segments the part and takes the centroid of its mask. However, high mask overlap predicts the pointing direction poorly when a part is spread out relative to its distance from the object center. Language-guided part segmentation is trained and evaluated by mask overlap, which leaves a gap between the masks it produces and the part geometry that these tasks use. We close this gap by learning point evidence, weights over the observed points whose weighted centroid gives the part's location. Geometric Readout Supervision (GRS) trains these weights through the centroid's position and adds direction supervision on the parts used in reorientation commands. GRS improves both tasks even with a single frozen encoder and a single input pose. We implement this supervision in GeoRead3D, a system for pointing and reorientation from part descriptions. A language model maps each description to a known part name. Dual-Route Part Evidence combines refined segmentation evidence with directly predicted point evidence to locate each part. On PartNeXt and Artiverse, GeoRead3D points within $22.5^\circ$ for 74.1% and 60.0% of queries, 5.8 and 7.3 percentage points above the strongest baselines. Its reorientation success exceeds the strongest baselines by 10.4 and 7.8 points.



### VeriOrigin: A Proactive Image Protection Framework for Provenance, Tamper Localization, and Lossless Recovery
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30523v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30523v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 11:39:22+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 11:39:22+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiale Chen, Wei William Wang, Junnan Yin, Chongyang Shi, Li Dong, Yuanman Li, Xiping Hu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Versatile watermarking, which seamlessly integrates provenance and tamper detection, has recently attracted widespread attention. In this paper, we propose \textbf{VeriOrigin}, a comprehensive and unified image protection framework that not only robustly achieves provenance and highly precise tamper localization under various complex distortions, but also guarantees perfect recovery of the original cover in lossless channels. To realize this framework, we depart from conventional flow based architectures. Instead, we design \textbf{XOnet}, a CNN based invertible encoder. XOnet comprises two standard CNN based watermarking sub networks, Xnet and Onet, which achieve reversibility through cross residual prediction. Based on XOnet, we train the proactive protection scheme via a two stage strategy: first optimizing the watermarking branch, followed by the tamper localization branch. During inference, the cover image undergoes sequential watermarking and tamper residual embedding. Extensive experimental results on four datasets demonstrate that VeriOrigin achieves precise tamper localization and strong robustness, while guaranteeing lossless cover recovery for integrity authentication. Project page is available at https://veriorigin.github.io/



### Assembly-JEPA: Using World Models for Fine-Grained Industrial Image Verification
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30530v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30530v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 11:42:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 11:42:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Michael Hollmer, Andreas Fischer
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Automated verification of industrial assembly processes remains a challenging problem due to substantial visual variability, limited availability of labeled defect data, and the fine-grained nature of many assembly errors. Existing approaches often rely on object detection, CAD models, or supervised classification pipelines, which can struggle to generalize across changing products and assembly configurations. In this work, we propose a novel self-supervised assembly verification framework based on Joint Embedding Predictive Architectures (JEPAs). Starting from the JEPA-based world model LeWorldModel, we investigate how the architecture must be adapted to verify the correctness of fine-grained assembly operations. To this end, we formulate assembly verification as a predictive world modeling task operating on before-and-after observations of assembly processes. Experimental results on industrial assembly tasks demonstrate that the proposed approach reliably detects both fine-grained incorrect assembly states and action-state mismatches, achieving an AUROC of 86.9% on a newly introduced assembly verification dataset. These results indicate that JEPA-based predictive representations constitute a promising foundation for robust and data-efficient industrial assembly verification.



### ReDiffDet: Rotation-equivariant Diffusion Model for Oriented Object Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30531v1
- **DOI**: 10.1109/CVPR52734.2025.02275
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30531v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 11:43:09+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 11:43:09+00:00
- **Authors**: Zeyu Ding, Yong Zhou, Jiaqi Zhao, Hancheng Zhu, Wen-Liang Du, Rui Yao
- **Comment**: CVPR 2025
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The diffusion model has been successfully applied to various detection tasks. However, it still faces several challenges when used for oriented object detection: objects that are arbitrarily rotated require the diffusion model to encode their orientation information; uncontrollable random boxes inaccurately locate objects with dense arrangements and extreme aspect ratios; oriented boxes result in the misalignment between them and image features. To overcome these limitations, we propose ReDiffDet, a framework that formulates oriented object detection as a rotation-equivariant denoising diffusion process. First, we represent an oriented box as a 2D Gaussian distribution, forming the basis of the denoising paradigm. The reverse process can be proven to be rotation-equivariant within this representation and model framework. Second, we design a conditional encoder with conditional boxes to prevent boxes from being randomly placed across the entire image. Third, we propose an aligned decoder for alignment between oriented boxes and image features. The extensive experiments demonstrate ReDiffDet achieves promising performance and significantly outperforms the diffusion model baseline.



### BEND: Spectral Bregman Losses for Multi-Image Unlearning in Diffusion Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30532v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30532v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 11:43:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 11:43:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Mohamed-Hicham Leghettas, Markus Püschel
- **Comment**: 25 pages, 8 figures, 22 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Diffusion models memorize training images and reproduce them almost verbatim when prompted, creating copyright and privacy liabilities. Data unlearning erases the influence of specific training samples from a trained model without retraining, and is two-sided: it must forget the targets while preserving generation quality on everything else. Prior methods use the squared-L2 diffusion loss, at most with a hand-set frequency mask; however, it is known that every Bregman divergence is a mathematically valid choice and the squared-L2 is only the simplest example. BEND is the first to search this family: it fits a spectral Bregman divergence to the unlearning task through a bilevel loop, so the loss itself is shaped to forget thoroughly while protecting quality. It also redirects a memorized image to the mean of its retain images, where ReTrack, the state of the art, redirects it to a single near-copy. On Stable Diffusion, a single BEND model erases all 48 verifiably memorized benchmark images at once. It forgets at least as much as per-target, sequential, and jointly trained ReTrack (SSCD 0.084 versus 0.094-0.120), at the quality of the model before unlearning (CLIP-IQA 0.81 versus 0.64-0.69). BEND also outperforms the state of the art on CelebA-HQ in all three measures of the benchmark, and matches or exceeds it on MNIST and CIFAR-10.



### DelayOPSD: On-Policy Self-Distillation from a Synchronous Teacher for Real-time Asynchronous Policies
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30534v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30534v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 11:43:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 11:43:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Hyeonjae Kim, Jeongsol Kim, Sunwoo Park, Chanyu Moon, Yoojin Oh, Youngjun Jun, Kyumin Choi, Jong Chul Ye
- **Comment**: 24 pages. Code and videos: https://github.com/present091/DelayOPSD
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Flow-based vision-language-action (VLA) policies incur substantial inference latency due to their large vision-language backbones and iterative denoising. Asynchronous execution reduces this latency by generating the next action chunk while executing the current one, but the new chunk is conditioned on a stale observation, degrading performance as the delay increases. We investigate this mismatch by comparing actions predicted from observations at planning and execution times. We find that these predictions differ little on expert demonstrations but diverge substantially on the policy's own delayed rollouts, particularly in failed episodes. Motivated by this observation, we propose DelayOPSD, which trains an asynchronous policy to imitate its synchronous counterpart on the policy's own rollouts. Specifically, a synchronous teacher, obtained from the same policy using the observation at execution time, supervises a delayed student that observes only the earlier frame and the actions still being executed. The student learns to match the teacher throughout its own denoising process. Under a 10-step delay, training SmolVLA on demonstrations achieves only 70.2% success, comparable to naive asynchronous execution (70.4%), whereas training on its own rollouts improves success to 78.7%. Across LIBERO and Kinetix, DelayOPSD achieves the highest average success among the compared methods, without introducing additional modules at deployment. On a real robot, it further improves success from 46% to 62%.



### Where Compositionality Hides: Shared-Content Interference in Vision-Language Retrieval
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30538v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30538v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 11:47:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 11:47:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Chimata Anudeep, Harsh Vardhan Poddar
- **Comment**: 18 pages, 7 figures, 10 tables. Code in ancillary files
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Paired compositional benchmarks such as Winoground ask each image to prefer its matching caption and each caption to prefer its matching image. A failed Group decision can come from a wrong caption preference or from the cross-image comparison alone, and Group Score merges the two. Choosing between retraining the encoder and changing the scoring rule therefore requires separating them. We study mean-erasure, which projects each frozen image embedding away from the mean of the two normalized candidate captions. We prove that the projection preserves every within-image caption ranking and makes Group Score equal baseline Text Score. On Winoground, mean-erasure raises CLIP ViT-B/32 Group Score from 9% to 31%, and five CLIP and SigLIP checkpoints gain 21 to 27 percentage points. ColorSwap shows a similar gain for color-object binding. A score decomposition shows that about 60% of Image failures already score the correct joint assignment above the swapped one and fail only on an offset between the two images. Fixed corrections and a learned bilinear scorer gain under one point. A direction estimated from retrieved captions gains about five points with two captions and falls below baseline with sixteen. Before retraining an encoder for paired retrieval, measure the Group loss that mean-erasure recovers: gains beyond baseline Text Score require changing the caption decisions themselves.



### SVUBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Proactive Streaming Video Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30541v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30541v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 11:48:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 11:48:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuxuan Liang, Bohan Zhang, Haiyang Yu, Bocheng Hu, Ke Niu, Xu Li, Zhengtao Yao, Yan Wen, Haotian Chen, Xiaolei Chen, Jiacong Wang, Zhe Liu, Jingqun Tang, Benlei Cui, Fan Shi, Xiangyang Xue
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Proactive streaming video understanding requires models to interpret unfolding events, respond timely, and retrieve relevant historical evidence. To systematically evaluate these capabilities, we introduce SVUBench, a comprehensive proactive streaming video benchmark comprising 12,131 questions and 62,881 answer points across ten proactive tasks spanning Perception, Understanding, and Reasoning. Constructed through model-assisted annotation and human verification, SVUBench introduces two complementary interaction modes. Basic evaluates sustained proactive responses from video onset, while Trajectory requires historical recovery and continuous response updates following task activation during streaming. A Duration-Scaled Challenge further evaluates these capabilities across four video duration tiers. We develop the Proactive Response Score (PRS) with task-adaptive answer windows to jointly assess answer correctness, response timeliness, historical consistency, and content quality. Extensive experiments and analysis reveal the strengths and limitations of existing models, highlighting directions for advancing proactive streaming video understanding.



### RAPID: Codec-Aware Video Decoding for Efficient Long-Video Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30557v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.MM
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30557v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:02:25+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:02:25+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuna Lee, Yulhwa Kim
- **Comment**: 16 pages, 16 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent vision-language models (VLMs) can process hour-long videos, but the large number of input frames incurs substantial inference overhead. Existing approaches to efficient long-video understanding largely overlook video decoding, which can take longer than LLM prefill. We propose RAPID (Reference- and Access-Point-Informed Decoding), a codec-aware framework that exploits information embedded in compressed bitstreams to reduce both frame decoding and selection costs. RAPID consists of two components. RAPID-Dec exploits reference-frame information and random access points to skip unnecessary frames and decode the retained frames in parallel while preserving exact reconstruction of the target frames. It achieves up to a 3.7x decoding speedup over the fastest baseline on H.264/AVC videos. RAPID-Sel uses keyframes as coarse representatives of video segments, avoiding the dense candidate decoding and scoring required by existing frame selection methods. It achieves comparable accuracy with up to a 3.0x preprocessing speedup. Together, RAPID achieves a 4.9x time to first token (TTFT) speedup on hour-long videos over a state-of-the-art frame selection method using the fastest baseline decoder.



### JONES-19: A Cultural Image Dataset Based on The Grammar of Ornament
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30558v1
- **DOI**: 10.1145/3769526.3769634
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30558v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:03:54+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:03:54+00:00
- **Authors**: Linh Pham, Stuart Shieber, David Alvarez-Melis, Alexandros Haridis
- **Comment**: 13 pages. Published in the Proceedings of the 22nd International Conference on Culture and Computer Science (KUI '25), Berlin, Germany, September 2025. Dataset available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/harvardseas-cultural-ornaments/JONES-19
- **Journal**: Proceedings of the 22nd International Conference on Culture and Computer Science (KUI '25), ACM, 2025, pp. 1-10
- **Summary**: We introduce JONES-19, a high-quality image dataset documenting 1,901 ornament designs belonging to nineteen human cultures. The images and their annotations are based on an open access archive of The Grammar of Ornament (London, 1856), by Owen Jones. The dataset poses numerous challenges as a benchmark for computer vision classification tasks and for research at the intersection of machine learning and art and design: a small sample size, image samples of human-designed artifacts rather than common objects in-context or natural scenes, imbalanced class distribution, and image distinctions based on fine details involving line patterns, reliefs, and colors. As a design-inspired dataset, JONES-19 can serve as a benchmark for various research fields, such as visual recognition, data-efficient learning, art-historical research, architectural style analysis, and cultural heritage. This paper describes the curation of the JONES-19 dataset and reports a baseline classification benchmark that evaluates the suitability of the dataset for training classifiers and exposes insights into inter-class relationships--particularly cultural similarities--by examining patterns in misclassification errors. The dataset and its accompanying documentation are available at: https://huggingface.co/datasets/harvardseas-cultural-ornaments/JONES-19.



### Seeing Is Locating: Spatial Awareness and Geographic Bias in Decision Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30564v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30564v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:09:31+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:09:31+00:00
- **Authors**: Wilson Wongso, Hada Melino Muhammad, Xiachong Lin, Hao Xue, Flora D. Salim
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Decision models, which output probability distributions over candidate labels instead of generating text, are increasingly used as classifiers and evaluators for inputs associated with real-world locations. However, it remains unclear whether they can leverage spatial context and whether their predictions are geographically biased. We introduce GeoDecisions, a multimodal benchmark for evaluating the spatial awareness and geographic bias of decision models. GeoDecisions comprises two tasks: (1) geo-aware image classification, and (2) location rating on objective and subjective topics. Evaluating six decision models against supervised task-specific models, we find that spatial awareness requires vision: textual coordinates alone provide insufficient context for classifying everyday photos and satellite images. We also find substantial geographic bias. On everyday photos, despite achieving higher accuracy, open-weight decision models exhibit more spatially clustered errors than supervised models. Moreover, across all five subjective topics, every decision model gives lower ratings to locations with lower socioeconomic conditions. Finally, we propose a training-free method that combines decision models with a location prior to improve their spatial awareness without substantially increasing performance bias. Our code is available at https://github.com/cruiseresearchgroup/GeoDecisions.



### Reliable Recursive Memory for Causal Multi-scale Video Polyp Segmentation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30568v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30568v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:14:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:14:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Tong Wang, Wenbo Peng, Yaolei Qi, Yuting He, Yutong Xie, Guanyu Yang
- **Comment**: 13 pages, 9 figures, 9 tables. Extended version of CMSA-Net (MICCAI 2026). Submitted to Medical Image Analysis
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video polyp segmentation (VPS) is essential for computer-aided colonoscopy, as it localizes and tracks polyps frame by frame. However, VPS remains challenging because polyps resemble the mucosa and change quickly in scale, which calls for history beyond a short clip, and because blur, specular highlights, and occlusion often degrade only part of a frame. Existing methods keep history as whole frames, so their cost grows with the history length, and a partly degraded frame carries its degraded content to later predictions. To address these issues, we propose STREAM, a streaming framework for VPS that extends the causal multi-scale aggregation (CMA) of our conference work from a fixed clip to an online stream. STREAM introduces a Recursive Slot Memory (RSM) that summarizes multi-scale features and soft foreground and background cues into a constant number of slots, which the current features read and pass to CMA, supplying long-range history at a constant cost. Furthermore, we design Reliability-Aware Slot Writing (RASW), which estimates a pixel-level quality map from the current frame, supervised by its local error, and uses it to control which locations are written into RSM and how much each slot is updated. Experiments on SUN-SEG and CVC-ClinicDB demonstrate that STREAM achieves state-of-the-art performance, improving the Dice of the conference model from 81.3% to 85.7% on the hardest subset while running at 48 FPS with a constant state of 0.32 MB. Code will be released at https://github.com/wangtong627/STREAM.



### Write, Evolve, Read: Streaming Cameras into a Persistent World State for 4D Occupancy Forecasting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30573v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30573v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:17:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:17:40+00:00
- **Authors**: ZiAn Wang, Tianyi Wang, Zhihao Chen, Manni Cui, Junkai Chen, ZhiJin Chen, Ruiqi Liu, Junfei Wu, Huanyu Zhang, Shu Wu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 4D occupancy forecasting predicts the 3D semantic occupancy of a driving scene seconds ahead. Over a few seconds, the scene changes little while the vehicle moves, so most of the space ahead has already been seen. Sorting the future into unchanged, changed, and unseen space, we find that current camera-based world models fall short on the unchanged part: they fail to hold on to what they have already seen, rather than to forecast what will change. We therefore make what the model has seen its state. PersistWorld streams camera frames into a persistent 3D world state anchored in the scene, writing each frame once instead of rebuilding the state from past frames. The state is learned end to end with the forecast, and an evolver predicts it at any queried time along the ego trajectory. On Occ3D-nuScenes, PersistWorld raises the IoU of the best camera-based world model by 19 points at 1-3 s and by 18 to 24 points up to 8 s, while running more than twice as fast. Code and models: https://github.com/wangzian828/PersistWorld. Project page: https://wangzian828.github.io/PersistWorld



### A Search Worth a Billion Pixels: Explaining Gigapixel Classifiers
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30578v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30578v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:21:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:21:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Danilo Danese, Angela Lombardi, Tommaso Di Noia
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Perturbation-based attribution methods explain model predictions without accessing their internal representations, but scaling them to high-resolution images requires balancing perturbation granularity and computational cost. Fine-grained perturbations require many model evaluations, whereas coarse masks may overlook small but relevant evidence. We introduce Wavelet Find-and-Refine (WaFR), a model-agnostic attribution method combining adaptive hierarchical search with sparse multiscale regression. By selectively refining regions that influence the prediction, WaFR maintains fine-grained localisation with a computational cost that depends on the spatial distribution of the evidence. As image resolution increases to 8,192 pixels per side, WaFR maintains a Pointing Game score of at least .995 in controlled localisation experiments, including at matched forward-pass budgets. On gigapixel whole-slide images, it requires a median of 176 forward passes and achieves the highest mean patch-level AUROC among the evaluated perturbation-based methods (.949).



### Foundation Model and Radiomics Distance Scores for Post-Hoc Segmentation Failure Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30582v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30582v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:24:36+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:24:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Simon Johannes Joham, Gianluca Guglielmo, Mateusz Kozinski, Martin Urschler
- **Comment**: Accepted at the UNSURE MICCAI 2026 Workshop
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Medical image segmentation models can fail silently when clinical data differs from training data. Deployed models rarely ship with quality control, and are often black boxes. Prior methods that work in this post-hoc failure detection setting still need a separate regression or segmentation model, registration, or access to model internals. We instead propose two failure scores based on the Mahalanobis distance to a labeled reference set. The first uses radiomic shape features of the predicted mask. The second uses frozen foundation model (FM) features, made mask-aware by overlaying the mask on the image, encoding it with a frozen DINOv3, and pooling patch features with more weight on the mask region. We test on multi-site prostate and hippocampus MRI under imaging-protocol and population shift. Ranking cases by predicted failure risk within each site, both scores are competitive with the registration-based (Atlas-RCA) and ensemble-based (pairwise Dice) baselines, without registration that can fail silently or access to individual predictions. Our results show that pooling all test cases into one ranking, as prior work does, rewards ordering sites by difficulty, so a detector can rank sites well, miss failures within them, and still look strong. Within-site evaluation measures true per-case failure. The two evaluation schemes reward different behavior, so we recommend reporting both. The code is available at https://github.com/imigraz/fm-radiomics-failure-detection.



### DARE: Dual-Level Adversarial Learning with Domain-Aware Regularization for Whole Slide Image Classification
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30589v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30589v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:29:19+00:00

- **Authors**: Yuxuan Jiang, Haichuan Dong, Manning Wang
- **Comment**: Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. 22 pages, 7 figures, including the appendix
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pathological whole slide images (WSIs) exhibit substantial domain shifts across hospitals due to variations in scanning devices, staining protocols, and tissue preparation procedures, leading to significant performance degradation when models are applied to data from different sources. Although unsupervised domain adaptation (UDA) has shown strong performance in transferring models from a labeled source domain to an unlabeled target domain in natural images, it remains relatively underexplored in computational pathology. The ultra-high resolution of WSIs and their pronounced inter-slide and intra-slide heterogeneity pose unique challenges for domain adaptation, often rendering existing UDA methods unstable and susceptible to negative transfer. To address these challenges, we propose Dual-level Adversarial learning with domain-aware REgularization (DARE), a plug-and-play UDA framework for WSI classification. DARE incorporates adaptive pseudo-labeling within a teacher-student architecture to provide stable supervision for the target domain, alongside adversarial alignment of both patch-level and slide-level embeddings. Together, these components enable the joint optimization of semantic representations at both levels for cross-domain alignment. We further introduce domain-aware attention masking to regularize attention and improve cross-domain generalization. Extensive experiments on four public WSI datasets demonstrate that DARE consistently outperforms state-of-the-art approaches across various domain shifts.



### HOSE: Hierarchical Orthogonal Subspace Erasure for Concept Unlearning in Diffusion Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30592v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30592v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:31:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:31:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Xiaoqi Wei, Li Li, Kejiang Chen, Hanzhou Wu, Xinpeng Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Text-to-image diffusion models can generate harmful or sensitive content, such as pornographic or violent imagery, raising serious security concerns. Model editing has recently emerged as an efficient approach to mitigate this issue. It modifies pretrained models with minimal parameter updates to alter specific concepts while preserving overall model capabilities. However, due to the entangled representations of different concepts in diffusion models, direct modifications inevitably disrupt non-target concepts. Meanwhile, U-Net's hierarchical heterogeneity hinders unified strategies from balancing erasure and quality. To address these issues, we propose Hierarchical Orthogonal Subspace Erasure (HOSE). This method projects parameter updates into the subspace orthogonal to general knowledge, thereby eliminating interference with non-target concepts. Considering the hierarchical heterogeneity of the U-Net architecture, we further propose a hierarchical adaptive mechanism, constructing differentiated subspace projections for different layers to precisely adapt to the model's hierarchical structure, thereby resolving the inherent trade-off between erasure and image quality in unified constraints. Experimental results demonstrate the proposed method reduces harmful content generation by 80% compared with the unedited model. While it inevitably incurs some image quality degradation, it still outperforms other editing-based baselines in terms of quality preservation, and achieves a significantly better trade-off between harmful content suppression and quality loss than these baselines. Notably, our approach demonstrates higher adversarial robustness than existing SOTA method, indicating that the target concepts have been more thoroughly erased. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/sevenqiiu/HOSE.



### Advancements in Diabetic Retinopathy Classification Using Novel Multi-Stacked U-Net Architectures
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30593v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30593v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:31:33+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:31:33+00:00
- **Authors**: Ameya Uppina, S Navaneetha Krishnan, Talluri Krishna Sai Teja, Joe Dhanith P R
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Diabetes leads to several health complications throughout the body, with the complication in the eyes being Diabetic Retinopathy. It leads to damaging or abnormal changes in the blood vessels' structure, causing vision loss. The need for massive screening of a large population of diabetic patients has created a necessity for computer-aided, fully automatic diagnosis of DR. CNNs have shown great promise in the classification of DR based on the Fundus images. Building a reliable model faces considerable challenges due to the limited availability of high-quality annotated datasets and issues with varying image quality and class imbalances. This paper aims to demonstrate the UNET, and its Stacked Architecture variant and their performance across several datasets. This paper has compared and studied the UNET architecture's performance across multiple stacks and has come to a soft conclusion that the architecture performs its best at three stacks, and any more leads to deterioration of its performance. The model aims at classifying the images into five categories, 0 to 4, where 0 is no DR, and 4 is proliferative DR.



### ViDiHand2: Unifying Video Diffusion Priors and Hand Geometry for 3D Hand Motion Reconstruction in the Wild
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30597v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30597v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:33:36+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:33:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuxi Wang, Yufei Liu, Chengkai Jin, Qingyue Chen, Wenqi Ouyang, Tianyi Wei, Kerui Ren, Siyuan Huang, Zhiqi Shen, Hongsheng Li, Xingang Pan
- **Comment**: Main text 8 pages with supplementary material, 54 pages in total
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Learning robot manipulation from egocentric human videos requires accurate metric 3D hand motion. Recent methods leverage pretrained video diffusion model (VDM) priors to recover occluded hands directly from full-frame videos, but their appearance-based depth regression generalizes poorly to unseen cameras. In this work, we propose ViDiHand2, a geometry-driven framework that reformulates VDM-based hand depth estimation from direct regression into fine-grained geometric constraint solving. Specifically, we introduce Fine-Grained Geometric Lifting and Clip-Level Ray Fitting, which recover metric depth by enforcing geometric consistency between pixel-aligned 2D joints and the metric structure of MANO hands, while leveraging temporal observations across the clip. Since the lifting relies on these 2D joints, to improve robustness under occlusion and image truncation, we further develop Confidence-Gated Fusion to adaptively combine geometrically lifted joints with MANO predictions according to localization confidence. Finally, we enhance the VDM backbone to better capture spatial-temporal representations and produce the per-frame 2D joint and MANO predictions that both steps rely on. Extensive experiments on seven public datasets demonstrate that ViDiHand2 consistently outperforms 18 baselines, reducing in-the-wild absolute joint error by 42% and detection failures by 8.82x compared with the best baseline on each metric, while running at 57 FPS. The code is available at https://github.com/NTUYWANG103/ViDiHand.



### GSDet: Gaussian Splatting for Oriented Object Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30602v1
- **DOI**: 10.24963/ijcai.2025/101
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30602v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:39:05+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:39:05+00:00
- **Authors**: Zeyu Ding, Jiaqi Zhao, Yong Zhou, Wen-liang Du, Hancheng Zhu, Rui Yao
- **Comment**: IJCAI 2025
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Oriented object detection has advanced with the development of convolutional neural networks (CNNs) and transformers. However, modern detectors still rely on predefined object candidates, such as anchors in CNN-based methods or queries in transformer-based methods, which struggle to capture spatial information effectively. To address the limitations, we propose GSDet, a novel framework that formulates oriented object detection as Gaussian splatting. Specifically, our approach performs detection within a 3D feature space constructed from image features, where 3D Gaussians are employed to represent oriented objects. These 3D Gaussians are projected onto the image plane to form 2D Gaussians, which are then transformed into oriented boxes. Furthermore, we optimize the mean, anisotropic covariance, and confidence scores of these randomly initialized 3D Gaussians, using a decoder that incorporates 3D Gaussian sampling. Moreover, our method exhibits flexibility, enabling adaptive control and a dynamic number of Gaussians during inference. Experiments on 3 datasets indicate that GSDet achieves AP$_{50}$ gains of 0.7\% on DIOR-R, 0.3\% on DOTA-v1.0, and 0.55\% on DOTA-v1.5 when evaluated with adaptive control and outperforms mainstream detectors.



### Towards an Armenian Epigraphic Corpus
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30603v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30603v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:40:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:40:28+00:00
- **Authors**: Chahan Vidal-Gor{è}ne, Arpine Avetisyan, Edita Matevosyan, Seda Kirakosyan, Victoria Khurshudyan, Pollet Samvelian
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The Divan hay vimagrut'yan (1960-2017), the printed corpus of Armenian inscriptions, comprises nearly 7,600 records; a growing number of the inscriptions they describe, damaged or destroyed in recent decades, survive only in its pages. Armenian epigraphy nevertheless lacks a digital resource of the kind available for Greek, Latin or Georgian. This paper presents the Corpus Inscriptionum Armenicarum Electronicum, which turns the ten volumes of the Divan into an open digital corpus. Layout detection, OCR and a vision-language model produce a first structured reading of each record, which specialists then check on a proofreading platform. First corrections show that letters are read reliably and that residual errors concentrate in the editorial signs. The corpus is designed as a lasting platform for documenting Armenian inscriptions and as a source of silver data in Classical and Middle Armenian for language models and computational linguistics.



### Tri-DNO: Trilateral Diffusion Noise Optimization for 3D Human Object Interaction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30607v1
- **DOI**: 10.1145/3828644.3857001
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30607v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:44:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:44:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Paul Hinzer, Niklas Gard, Anna Hilsmann, Peter Eisert
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Generating realistic 3D human object interactions (HOI) is essential for applications, such as augmented or virtual reality (AR/VR) or character animation of interactive entertainment. A particular challenge lies in the faithful generation of hand grasps in human object interaction scenes, where even minor geometric inconsistencies, such as insufficient contact or interpenetration, can significantly compromise realism. While diffusion-based generative models have shown promising results in HOI synthesis, ensuring physically plausible hand-object interactions remains challenging.   We present Tri-DNO, a diffusion noise optimization strategy to sample from a pretrained multimodal HOI diffusion model, improving hand-object contact. Rather than modifying or retraining the generative model, our approach iteratively optimizes the initial noise of the diffusion generator, thereby generating firm hand object contact, while mitigating penetration and keeping the hand position realistic. Experiments on the GRAB dataset demonstrate improvements over the baseline in grasp quality without additional model training or increased computational cost compared to existing methods. We further demonstrate the applicability of our approach to practical media production scenarios.



### CineAct: Interaction-State Agents for Playable, Branching Film Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30613v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30613v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:49:13+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:49:13+00:00
- **Authors**: Junyan Lin, Jianqi Ma, Shujie Duan, Hanxin Zhu, Zhihao Hu, Jinming Liu, Shuai Li, Jun Zhang, Xin Jin, Chang Wen Chen, Weidong Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Open video generators such as MiniMax-H3 produce short clips of only several to a dozen seconds per run. To synthesize longer sequences, existing methods either rely on agentic systems following preset scripts, or roll out autoregressively from the previous clip's last frame, losing off-screen states and accumulating hallucinations across turns. Consequently, users still cannot take an explicit goal, choose how to accomplish it, and see that choice persist across subsequent clips. We study goal-grounded interactive video generation, where one goal admits multiple valid approaches, the user's chosen approach must be executed on screen, and its consequences must persist across clips. To this end, we propose CineAct, a state-first agentic framework in which language-model agents handle reasoning while frozen image and video generators serve purely as renderers. Before interaction, the Architect initializes the world as a spatial layout with cameras, an asset library, and a scene graph; at each turn, the system deduces the planned state change, generates a target end frame with an image generator and rigorously verifies it, and synthesizes intermediate motion with an FL2V model. We further introduce a multi-space benchmark covering two suites: long-horizon step execution and open-intent branching admissibility. Experiments show that explicit world-state modeling boosts multi-step rollout consistency over the pixel-only baseline (+11.4 automated, +23.1 human points) and raises paired branching success toward a shared goal by +30.8 points.



### Auxiliary Clues Aware's Geometry Problem Solving
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30617v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30617v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 12:51:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 12:51:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Qigong Lei, Qingsong Wang, Wang Lin, Qianyi Yang, Chang Yao, Jingyuan Chen
- **Comment**: Accepted by NeurIPS2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Geometry Problem Solving (GPS) serves as a rigorous test of the complex reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), particularly when solutions require auxiliary constructions absent from the input diagram. However, explicit diagramming methods suffer from unstable image generation quality and cascading errors, while existing implicit attention mechanisms primarily focus on visible elements, limiting their ability to support reasoning that requires additional geometric constructions. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{AuxCA} (Auxiliary-Clues-Aware GRPO), a reinforcement learning framework that helps models learn transferable auxiliary-construction strategies that can be applied beyond the training domain without explicit diagram generation at inference time. Specifically, we introduce Auxiliary Clues Dependency (\textbf{ACD}) to quantify the influence of auxiliary visual clues on token generation and integrate it into GRPO through fine-grained advantage weighting. Furthermore, we incorporate an auxiliary incentive reward to encourage the effective use of auxiliary geometric clues in correct solutions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AuxCA-8B outperforms the explicit-plotting baseline Bagel-Canvas and achieves competitive performance with leading proprietary models on auxiliary-dependent plane geometry problems. Improvements on analytic geometry, solid geometry, and external benchmarks further suggest that the learned strategies can transfer beyond the training domain. Code is available at https://github.com/fukua23/AuxCA



### SWEEP-Bench: Do Embodied Policies Act on the Speed They See?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30637v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30637v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:03:17+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:03:17+00:00
- **Authors**: Hiroaki Honda, Koya Sakamoto, Shu Morikuni, Yusuke Iwasawa, Taiki Miyanishi
- **Comment**: 28 pages, 24 figures. Project page: https://damin3927.github.io/SWEEP-Bench
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Robot policies must adapt to the speed of moving objects, yet existing benchmarks rarely measure this ability across diverse tasks and speeds. We introduce SWEEP-Bench, a simulation benchmark with 80 conveyor-belt manipulation tasks spanning 16 skill categories, each evaluated at 13 belt speeds (0.10-0.40 m/s). Objects are transported through physical contact with a visibly moving belt. Each task has a static counterpart, and each evaluation episode uses the same randomized scene at all 13 speeds. We fine-tune eight vision-language-action and world-action models on demonstrations spanning the full speed range, comparing observations from the current frame alone with those including two past frames. For six models, the success rate peaks at intermediate speeds, and their failures indicate that they tend to produce actions suited to the average demonstration speed rather than to the actual one. Past frames raise the success rate by 10-40 percentage points for three models, and linear probes show that the belt speed is also most linearly decodable from these three. Adding ground-truth speed input to four of the models improves all of them, and a speed estimated from the images keeps most of this gain. These results show that SWEEP-Bench exposes speed-dependent failures and suggest that past frames help only policies from which the speed is most decodable, such as those pretrained on multiple frames. Project page: https://damin3927.github.io/SWEEP-Bench



### Motion on Demand: Adaptive Optical Excitation for Event Cameras
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30641v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30641v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:08:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:08:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Yingtao Li, Chaoran Feng, Haoran Jiang, Zhenyu Tang, Wangbo Yu, Zhankuo Xu, Jianbin Zhao, Li Yuan, Yonghong Tian
- **Comment**: Project Page: https://passwerob.github.io/EvGaze/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Event cameras respond only to brightness changes, so whether an edge is visible depends on image motion perpendicular to it---a motion that constantly shifts with camera and scene dynamics. Rotating a wedge prism in front of the lens introduces controlled image motion to reveal static or slow edges. However, a fixed rotation speed cannot adapt to changing motion conditions: when natural motion is already sufficient, additional optical excitation generates redundant events and introduces unnecessary alignment errors. Inspired by the division of labor between fast haltere feedforward and slower visual feedback in fly gaze control, we present EvGaze, an event camera system that adaptively regulates prism rotation speed. EvGaze employs a learned multimodal controller that integrates luminance, event, and inertial observations, with a fast inertial pathway that refines speed decisions between visual updates. To preserve geometric consistency during speed changes, we compensate for prism-induced image motion at individual event timestamps using measured encoder angles. On EvMotion, EvGaze recovers more edges than full-speed prism rotation with 39% fewer events. On DSEC with simulated optical modulation, it reaches 0.73 feature-tracking success (compared with 0.55 on native events and 0.67 at 180 RPM) while generating 26% fewer events than 360 RPM. Real-device experiments further demonstrate online closed-loop adaptation, reducing average event rates by 17% relative to full-speed rotation.



### Beyond Naming Accuracy: Template Composition and Species-Name Decisions
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30642v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30642v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:08:27+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:08:27+00:00
- **Authors**: Junle li, Bochang zhang
- **Comment**: 29 pages, 10 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language classifiers identify species by matching images to text prompts containing species names. BioCLIP2 changes its predicted species less often than BioCLIP 2.5 Huge across four photographic templates, but the ranking reverses across a bank of 80 templates. This comparison leaves open whether the reversal depends on the number of templates or their wording. We hold the count at four and compare the classifiers on every four-template subset of the 80-template bank and of a second pool of 24 reviewed photographic templates, keeping images and candidate names fixed. Huge is more consistent across the reviewed pool as a whole, yet 19.09% of its four-template subsets favor Bio-CLIP2 on iWildCam and 5.10% do so on Rare Species. We then test whether both rankings hold on 5,487 frames from previously unused iWildCam sequences. Among 4,356 subsets containing two photo and two photograph templates, simultaneous 95% intervals support lower disagreement for Huge in 1,526 subsets and for BioCLIP2 in 43. Even with the same prompt count and noun mixture, the choice of sentences can determine which classifier appears more consistent.



### Part-wise Certified State Estimation of Articulated Objects from Passive Stereo
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30646v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30646v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:10:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:10:10+00:00
- **Authors**: Orion Desbonnet, Kei Higuchi, Alex Sykes, Ayush Deshmukh, Victor Leve, Joao Moura, Sethu Vijayakumar
- **Comment**: 11 pages, 4 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Robots in manufacturing often handle thin, hinged objects known from CAD models that change shape during the task, like folding cartons. Monitoring such tasks requires estimating the object's articulation state, its pose and the shape of any semi-deformable part. Learning-based estimators generalise across objects but lose precision while handling thin parts. Model-based fitting is precise but often needs accurate depth, a close initial estimate and a clean object mask. We present part-wise certification of an articulated object's state, estimated from passive stereo. Our estimator rivals the precision of the best model-based fitting method without using depth, a close initial estimate or a mask. We detect the corners that the CAD model defines in both stereo images and fit the articulated model to them. Geometric certificates, which we extend from rigid to articulated objects, then tell whether to trust each part, because the fit returns a state even for hidden parts. Certified estimates on unlabelled video become labels that improve sim-to-real transfer. On five of seven simulated objects, we locate the parts we certify three to five times more precisely than the strongest learned baseline on the same readings, given depth and true masks. On a real folding carton, our median corner error on the end panels is 2.1% of the carton's size, at least 40% lower than every learned baseline and also lower than a depth-based fit initialised near the true state.



### Active Observation for Robust Visual Servoing in Robotic Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30658v1
- **DOI**: 10.1109/CASE69030.2026.11704293
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30658v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:21:01+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:21:01+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhilun Hu, Yuqi Cheng, Yuxiang Tan, Wenpan Li, Weiming Shen
- **Comment**: 6 pages, 4 figures, 3 tables. Published in 2026 IEEE 22nd International Conference on Automation Science and Engineering (CASE), pp. 3026-3031
- **Journal**: 2026 IEEE 22nd International Conference on Automation Science and Engineering (CASE), Shenyang, China, 2026, pp. 3026-3031
- **Summary**: Robust visual servoing during robotic grasping remains challenging, as approach-phase occlusion frequently disrupts closed-loop control. To address this, we present an active observation framework in which one arm of an existing dual-arm platform carries an RGB-D camera as an active observer while the other executes grasping. The observer repositions to maintain or recover a shared target-gripper view within the feasible shared-view workspace. Occlusion during the final approach is particularly acute: fixed eye-to-hand lacks an active recovery mechanism once the target is hidden, while eye-in-hand can suffer from self-occlusion and sensitivity to hand-eye calibration. The observer first uses an active two-phase viewpoint strategy to align high-precision RGB-D axes with task-relevant error components. If visibility later degrades, an occlusion-triggered exploration module pauses the worker arm and scores candidate viewpoints by observation quality to recover a shared target-gripper view. Because both objects are detected in a single RGB-D image, the resulting Relative Servo Error avoids differencing two independently transformed target and gripper estimates. We evaluate the system on a dual 6-DoF Piper platform using an open-vocabulary detector with text prompts, without task-specific fine-tuning or demonstrations. Across 50 randomized trials per object, the system achieves 94% grasp success versus 76% for a reactive viewpoint baseline and 64% for a fixed camera. On triggered severe-occlusion trials, recovery takes $1.8 \pm 0.6$ s on average.



### WeMM-Ecom: Advancing Multimodal Content Understanding for WeChat E-Commerce
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30662v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30662v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:22:22+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:22:22+00:00
- **Authors**: Binhui Xie, Jinmin Li, Haomiao Tang, Guangting Wang, Song Jin, Jianwei Zhu, Fengyun Rao, Jing Lyu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We present WeMM-Ecom, a family of multimodal large language models that deliver frontier e-commerce capabilities while achieving state-of-the-art general-purpose performance at their respective scales. The family spans five sizes, from a 0.8B dense model to a 35B mixture-of-experts model with 3B active parameters. Our training pipeline begins with two-stage continued pre-training that progressively raises the proportion of e-commerce data within a diverse multimodal data mixture, followed by hybrid-thinking post-training that combines on-policy distillation and reinforcement learning to support both non-thinking and thinking modes. For evaluation, we construct a comprehensive suite of 28 e-commerce benchmarks (23 in-house and five open-source) covering eight business scenarios and complement it with 64 public general-purpose benchmarks. On the e-commerce suite, WeMM-Ecom-35B-A3B surpasses substantially larger open-source flagship models, including Qwen3.8-Flash-Next, HY4, and DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash. On the general-purpose suite, every WeMM-Ecom model surpasses its similarly sized Qwen3.5 counterpart, with the lightweight variants (e.g., WeMM-Ecom-2B) showing clear advantages over leading lightweight models such as Qwen3.5-2B, MiniCPM-V-4.7-1B, and LFM3-VL-2B. Beyond benchmarks, WeMM-Ecom now powers hundreds of production use cases in WeChat, including catalog management, multimodal product search, and livestream commerce, serving billion-scale requests per day. We publicly release all model weights and detail our data curation, training recipe, and benchmark construction to facilitate future work on domain-specialized multimodal models. We have released the model weights and code to facilitate future research at https://github.com/Tencent/WeMM-Ecom.



### Zhengrong: World Models with Pole-Based Action-Response Factorization for Direct Visual Planning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30666v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30666v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:24:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:24:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Yu Zhang, Jingrui Li, Congyu Wang, Yuhang Cheng, Wenbo Chen, Xiaobo Zhang, Jing Zou, Yuxi Dai
- **Comment**: 16 pages, 4 figures, 11 tables. Technical report, Beijing Freedo Technology Co., Ltd
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Direct visual world models predict the effects of candidate action sequences without recursive latent rollout, yet they leave the temporal structure of action effects unspecified. We introduce Zhengrong (Zhengrong-PoleWM), a world model that decomposes a latent trajectory into an initial-condition anchor, a free response, and delayed responses to action innovations. Nonlinear networks map visual history and causal action prefixes to response coefficients. A shared bank of stable conjugate poles determines how each response evolves with elapsed time. This factorization preserves nonlinear interactions across actions, anchors predictions exactly at the initial state, prevents future actions from affecting earlier predictions, and yields bounded responses to finite control plans. We derive an exact re-anchoring identity that identifies the additional basis direction $Ψ_κ(s)$ needed to represent shifted trajectory tails. Across four offline image-goal control benchmarks (Cube, TwoRoom, PushT, and Reacher), Zhengrong-PoleWM achieves $90.83\% \pm 0.47\%$ macro-average success over two training seeds at epoch 20, exceeding the strongest baseline (QQWorld) by 6.08 percentage points. Selecting checkpoints by validation loss yields $91.21\% \pm 1.00\%$. In a controlled first-epoch Cube study without restart regularization, neural and pole response heads outperform a generic direct predictor by 20.67 and 23.34 percentage points, respectively. On an NVIDIA A100 GPU, median latency for a full 30-iteration cross-entropy method search ranges from 74 to 87 ms across the four tasks. The mean of these medians is 77.5 ms, a 15.8% latency reduction relative to Fast-LeWM and a $7.1\times$ speedup over autoregressive LeWM.



### DriveDreamer-RSI: Recursive Self-Improvement of Driving Perception and Agentic Data Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30671v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30671v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:28:20+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:28:20+00:00
- **Authors**: Dingyi Yao, Tingdong Yu, Guosheng Zhao, Chaojun Ni, Xiaofeng Wang, Jiming Mao, Guan Huang, Lihui Peng, Zheng Zhu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Controllable generation can expand training data for driving perception, but visual realism alone does not ensure learning gains. Effective generation must adapt both what data to generate and how to construct them as the perception model evolves. In this paper, we present DriveDreamer-RSI, a framework for recursive self-improvement of driving perception and agentic data generation through feedback between perception learning and data construction. Its outer loop implements Gap-Guided Target Adaptation, generating labeled videos from current perception gaps and updating targets after training. Its inner loop uses a vision-language agent to iteratively edit scenes through observation and execution feedback. Across rounds, Learning-Feedback-Driven Strategy Refinement derives reusable construction advice from execution experience and downstream learning outcomes. On the nuScenes validation set, DriveDreamer-RSI surpasses the dataset-replay baseline by 4.09 mAP and 4.13 NDS points using approximately half the synthetic frames.



### Optimising VLM Harnesses to Localise Errors in Generated Videos
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30673v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30673v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:28:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:28:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Ankit Yadav, Nikhil Cherian Kurian, Arpit Garg, Lingqiao Liu
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 6 tables, and 2 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Identifying subtle errors in generated videos is an important step toward automated video generation, where errors must be detected to assess and refine generated clips. Recent work shows that pretrained vision-language models (VLMs) can localise such errors, but their effectiveness depends strongly on the workflow used: choices about which frames to inspect, which tools and model calls to make, and how to combine predictions can lead to substantially different results. We study VLM harnesses for video error localisation. Instead of relying on manually designed harnesses, we propose Harness Search and Merge (HSM), an algorithm that automatically constructs them by searching over executable evaluation programs. An LLM orchestrator refines candidate harnesses independently on random training subsets, records findings in a memory, and constructively merges complementary candidates. We evaluate HSM on Spotlight, a recently proposed benchmark for localising errors in generated videos. HSM achieves 0.234 +- 0.012 F1, exceeding the strongest benchmark strategy (0.173 +- 0.016), and retains its lead on an unseen video generator (0.213 +- 0.008 versus 0.140 +- 0.003). On IPV-Bench, it achieves strong localisation performance with lower false-alarm rates than existing optimisation baselines. Together, these results show that searching over complete VLM harnesses can outperform existing evaluation strategies, transfer to an unseen video generator, and reduce false alarms on real videos.



### Speculate in the Right Order: Efficient Self-Speculative Decoding for Autoregressive Image Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30704v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30704v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:49:13+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:49:13+00:00
- **Authors**: Yihan Chen, Jintao Zhang, Jun Zhu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Self-speculative decoding accelerates autoregressive image generation by leveraging the same model for parallel drafting and sequential verification, without requiring an additional draft model. While existing methods primarily focus on improving drafting and verification strategies, recent advances in random-order autoregressive modeling introduce an additional degree of freedom: the generation order. In this work, we theoretically analyze how generation order affects self-speculative decoding efficiency, identifying two key factors: draft-target mismatch, which determines token rejection probabilities, and early-rejection cost, which reflects the higher cost of rejecting tokens earlier in a speculative round. Motivated by these insights, we propose Speculation-Aware Ordering (SAO), a training-free framework for offline optimization of generation orders. Instead of evaluating candidate orders through costly speculative decoding, SAO uses simple pairwise conditional-TV statistics to formulate a rejection-cost-aware objective and efficiently construct speculation-friendly generation orders offline. Extensive experiments across different backbones and speculative widths demonstrate that SAO improves decoding efficiency, with the additional benefit of improved generation quality. Furthermore, SAO provides complementary efficiency gains when combined with existing self-speculative decoding methods. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first work to identify generation order as a new optimization dimension for efficient self-speculative decoding in autoregressive image generation.



### Intra-Class Diversity Loss for Person Re-Identification
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30707v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30707v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:51:42+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:51:42+00:00
- **Authors**: Wei Zhou, BaXian Chen
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 5 figures, 2 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Person re-identification (ReID) aims to retrieve images of the same pedestrian across different cameras. However, the non-overlapping identities between training and testing sets, together with large intra-class variation, make it challenging to learn generalizable pedestrian representations. Most existing ReID loss functions mainly focus on intra-class compactness and inter-class separation, which may overlook useful intra-class differences. To address these issues, we propose a novel Intra-Class Diversity (ICD) loss that aims to maximize intra-class differences in classification-irrelevant directions. To the best of our knowledge, we are the first to introduce this class-weight-orthogonal diversity learning objective for person ReID. Specifically, based on the geometric relationship between the learned representation and its corresponding class-weight vector, we decompose each representation into the component along the class-weight direction and its orthogonal component. This decomposition provides a clear and interpretable geometric basis for modeling intra-class diversity. ICD then encour- ages larger differences among the orthogonal components of samples belonging to the same identity, while the classification loss maintains identity discrimination. The proposed ICD loss can be incorporated into existing ReID frameworks as an additional regularization term without modifying the feature extraction network. Extensive experiments on three widely used ReID datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of ICD and its complementarity with existing metric learning losses.



### LiveAudio: From Bidirectional to Autoregressive Diffusion for Real-Time Streaming Video-to-Audio Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30714v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.SD**, cs.CV, cs.MM, eess.AS
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30714v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 13:55:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 13:55:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuxuan Jiang, Zehua Chen, Yusheng Dai, Andong Wang, Weibei Dou, Jun Zhu
- **Comment**: 23 pages, 12 tables, 14 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video-to-audio generation synthesizes sound that matches a video in content and timing, with applications ranging from film post-production to live streaming, games and interactive world models. Interactive applications receive video continuously and need the sound to follow it in real time, yet most existing video-to-audio models are bidirectional and must observe an entire clip before producing any sound. We propose LiveAudio, a streaming video-to-audio framework that generates high-quality and synchronized audio in real time while the video is playing. In contrast to prior streaming models trained from scratch, LiveAudio converts a bidirectional model into a streaming generator so as to build on its audio quality and synchronization. LiveAudio generates audio block by block, and each block depends only on the video that has already been played. The streaming generator is obtained from the bidirectional model through progressive causal fine-tuning and distillation, so that each block needs only a few sampling steps. Experiments on VGGSound show that LiveAudio outperforms existing streaming models in audio quality and synchronization, and surpasses the bidirectional model it starts from in synchronization. On a single A100 GPU, it runs in real time with a real-time factor of 0.79 and a time to first frame of 143 ms. Audio samples are available at https://streamaudio.github.io.



### Co-speech Gesture Attribute Annotation with 3D Human Mesh Recovery
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30725v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30725v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 14:05:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 14:05:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Frances Yung, Muhammad Naufilr
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Co-speech gestures convey meaning alongside speech, but annotating their physical attributes in video is labour-intensive. We propose two approaches to automate annotation of ten gesture attributes using human mesh recovery (HMR), which estimates a full 3D body skeleton from a single video frame: AI-assisted geometrical rules and a graph convolutional network (SL-GCN). Both use pose estimates from SAM 3D Body, evaluated on 2,020 gestures from the GESRes dataset. For physically defined attributes such as height, arm swivel and movement speed, both methods achieve agreement with expert annotation comparable to that between human annotators. For attributes whose annotation requires higher-level multimodal contextual inference, performance remains lower. The rule-based approach requires substantially less annotated data, is fully transparent, and can be adapted to different annotation schemes, offering a practical and interpretable complement to data-driven methods



### Same Seed, Different Picture? Deciding Early at Low Resolution for Seed-Faithful Few-Step Flow Sampling
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30728v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30728v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 14:08:55+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 14:08:55+00:00
- **Authors**: Huiran Duan, Weilun Feng, Yuqi Li, Yingli Tian
- **Comment**: 43 pages, 27 figures, 38 tables. Project page: https://duanhuiran.github.io/DtR
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Few-step samplers make large text-to-image flow models fast, but from the same noise they no longer generate the image of the standard 50-step sampler. Subjects, attributes and layout change, not just fine detail. We trace this loss of seed fidelity to the start of sampling. The coarse part of the initial noise decides coarse structure first, while the noise level is still above 0.9. Few-step samplers cross this range in their first steps, which account for 25-52% of their distance to the 50-step reference. Because this decision is coarse, a half-resolution run from the same coarse noise can make nearly the same decision at a third of the cost. We therefore propose Decide-then-Render (DtR), a training-free sampler that decides the composition in a few low-resolution steps, rebuilds the full-resolution state with the seed's own noise and renders the details. On 4 large models, DtR runs 11-25$\times$ faster than the reference sampler. At each speed-up, it is closer to the reference and rated higher by ImageReward than every compared sampler, including few-step schedules calibrated per budget and published multi-resolution samplers. On FLUX.1-dev, it remains the most faithful sampler down to about $8\times$, ahead of caching accelerators. A user study on this model found DtR's samples closer to the reference in 86% of pairwise trials. DtR transfers to a held-out model and to video, and distilled models can serve as its renderers. Project link: https://duanhuiran.github.io/DtR



### EgoDepth-Bench: Benchmarking Metric Depth for Egocentric Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30734v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30734v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 14:12:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 14:12:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuan Gao, Dongchen Wei, Xinyang Lin, Shuhua Cao, Xueliang Guo, Zhuohan Li, Yihao Wang
- **Comment**: 8 pages, 5 figures, 6 tables. Project page: https://egodepth-bench.github.io/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We introduce EgoDepth-Bench, comprising 941,233 synchronized samples collected across 37 distinct physical environments. Each sample combines six calibrated RGB views with metric depth in the native fisheye view, reconstructed from five active-infrared stereo modules. A calibrated ball-bar confirms millimeter-level geometric accuracy, with a median error of 1.27 mm on a known sphere spacing. Environment-held-out splits enable evaluation of generalization to unseen environments. Across monocular, stereo, and multi-view models, the benchmark measures both depth accuracy and prediction coverage, including on hands and objects. Fine-tuning MapAnything on EgoDepth-Bench reduces metric AbsRel from 0.708 to 0.091 and scale-aligned AbsRel from 0.327 to 0.072 on unseen environments. These results demonstrate that EgoDepth-Bench provides effective supervision and enables rigorous evaluation of metric and relative depth estimation in egocentric manipulation. The project page provides the evaluation code and interactive RGB-depth sequences.



### Object-Centric Differentiable Logic Networks for Abstract Visual Reasoning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30736v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30736v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 14:13:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 14:13:10+00:00
- **Authors**: Hanne Say Eren, Ana Ezquerro, Ozan Özdenizci
- **Comment**: This work has been submitted to the IEEE for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Human visual intelligence enables efficient reasoning capabilities on abstract visual problems with bounded cognitive resources and minimal experience, and yields remarkable generalization to novel problems with its ability to draw relational analogies and modular abstractions. In contrast, deep learning models require exhaustive task-specific training on large datasets, making them prone to exploiting visual shortcuts and resulting in poor generalization. While neuro-symbolic approaches attempt to bridge this gap by decoupling perception from reasoning with dedicated computation units, existing solutions rely either on explicit rule supervision or parameter-heavy transformer architectures that reintroduce the computational inefficiencies they aim to solve. We introduce Slot-Dwn to address these limitations, a novel neuro-symbolic architecture that unifies unsupervised object-centric perception with logic-based symbolic reasoning. Our model utilizes a Multi-Head Differentiable Weightless Network (MH-DWN) that performs efficient reasoning entirely in Boolean space using look-up tables (LUTs). Our framework reduces visual perception complexity and introduces a LUT-based relational bottleneck with sparse input routing to minimize computational overhead. We evaluate Slot-Dwn across various abstract visual reasoning benchmark tasks targeting systematic rule generalization, sample-efficient anomaly detection, and multi-relational reasoning. We demonstrate that Slot-Dwn yields competitive performance with respect to the state-of-the-art, under extreme out-of-distribution regimes, while requiring up to 52% fewer trainable parameters, with significantly faster convergence.



### Learning Dense Head-Object Contact Estimation with Synthetic Data
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30751v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30751v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-23 14:25:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-23 14:25:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Daniel Sungho Jung, Kyoung Mu Lee
